Q1. The job takes 6 days when A and B work together. If A alone takes 18 days, in how many days can B alone finish it?
A. 24 days
B. 14 days
C. 9 days
D. 12 days
Answer: 9 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_individual_from_combined
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{6}-\frac{1}{18}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
B=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{6\times 18}{18-6}=9
\]
Answer: 9 days

---

Q2. 10 workers can build a road stretch in 15 days by working 8 hours a day. How many workers are needed to finish it in 20 days by working 6 hours a day?
A. 13 workers
B. 14 workers
C. 10 workers
D. 15 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_road_construction_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
10\times 15\times 8=M_2\times 20\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{10\times 15\times 8}{20\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

---

Q3. A can finish \(\frac{1}{3}\) of a work in 6 days. In how many days can A finish all of it?
A. 9 days
B. 2 days
C. 23 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 18 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_fraction_days
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=6\times \frac{3}{1}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=6\div \frac{1}{3}=18
\]
Answer: 18 days

---

Q4. A completes \(\frac{3}{8}\) of a job in 12 days. In how many days will A complete the whole job?
A. 96 days
B. 20 days

Difficulty: Medium

\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=12\times \frac{8}{3}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=32
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=12\div \frac{3}{8}=32
\]
Answer: 32 days

Q5. If a work is completed in 30 days and 2 days have passed, what fraction of the work is yet to be done?
A. 15/14
B. 1/15
C. 14/1
D. 14/15
Answer: 14/15
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_residual_work
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
R=1-\frac{2}{30}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
R=\frac{28}{30}=14/15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{30-2}{30}=14/15
\]
Answer: 14/15

---

Q6. A tank is filled for one hour at 12 litres per hour and then drained for one hour at 5 litres per hour, repeatedly. How many hours are needed to fill a 60-litre tank?
A. 16 1/3 hours
B. 19 3/5 hours
C. 12 1/3 hours
D. 18 1/3 hours
Answer: 16 1/3 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_alternating_fill_empty
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
W_{cycle}=12-5
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
60\ units,\ cycle=7
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=16\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=C_f+t_f
\]
Answer: 16 1/3 hours

---

Q7. One-fourth of a tank is filled. The pipe can fill a full tank in 12 hours. In how many hours will the remaining part be filled?
A. 7 hours
B. 9 hours
C. 13 hours
D. 7 1/5 hours
Answer: 9 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_partial_tank_initially_filled
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{1-f}{r_f}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
T=\frac{1-1/4}{1/12}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=(1-f)F
\]
Answer: 9 hours

---

Q8. Two workers work for equal time. Their wages are ₹800 and ₹1200. What is the ratio of work done by A and B?
A. 1200:800
B. 2:3
C. 2000:1200
D. 80:60
Answer: 2:3
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_efficiency_from_wages
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
W_A:W_B=E_A:E_B
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
E_A:E_B=800:1200
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
E_A:E_B=2:3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=2:3
\]
Answer: 2:3

---

Q9. A worker makes 15 toys per day for the first 5 days. After that, the worker makes 12 toys per day. How many days are needed to make 180 toys?
A. 13 3/4 days
B. 17 3/4 days
C. 14 3/4 days
D. 11 days
Answer: 13 3/4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_productivity_decay
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-r_1t_1}{r_2}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=5+\frac{180-15\times 5}{12}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=13.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+t_2
\]
Answer: 13 3/4 days

---

Q10. Out of 120 forms, 36 forms are already complete. The final worker completes 8 forms daily. How many days are needed for the rest?
A. 15 3/4 days
B. 10 1/2 days
C. 7 1/2 days
D. 14 1/2 days
Answer: 10 1/2 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_phase_state_reconstruction
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
x=\frac{W-W_1}{r}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
x=\frac{120-36}{8}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
x=10.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
x=\frac{W_l}{r}
\]
Answer: 10 1/2 days

---

Q11. A takes 12 days to finish a work and B takes 18 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 30 days
B. 7 1/5 days
C. 12 days
D. 15 days
Answer: 7 1/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(12,18)=36
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=36/12=3,\quad B=36/18=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{36}{3+2}=7.2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 18}{12+18}=7.2
\]
Answer: 7 1/5 days

---

Q12. A team has 3 men and 4 women. They finish 4, 3 boxes per person per day respectively. How many days are needed for 360 boxes?
A. 18 days
B. 17 days
C. 11 days
D. 15 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_worker_equivalence_men_women
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=\frac{360}{3\times 4+4\times 3}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 15 days

---

Q13. There are 720 ration packets in a camp. If 24 packets are used each day, how many days will they last?
A. 26 days
B. 30 days
C. 29 days
D. 36 days
Answer: 30 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_resource_rate_equivalence
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720}{24}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Answer: 30 days

Q14. 8 workers were assigned to a job. 3 workers left after 10 days. How many days late will the job finish?
A. 11 1/4 days
B. 11 1/2 days
C. 7 1/2 days
D. 9 1/2 days
Answer: 7 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_worker_removed_delay
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
D=T_{actual}-T_{planned}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
R=180-8\times 10
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
D=\frac{100}{5}-\frac{100}{8}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=T_a-T_p
\]
Answer: 7 1/2 days

Q15. A and B's efficiencies are 12:18. What is the ratio of days required by A and B separately?
A. 12:18
B. 30:18
C. 18:30
D. 3:2
Answer: 3:2
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_time_ratio_from_efficiency
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T_A:T_B=E_B:E_A
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T_A:T_B=18:12
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A:T_B=3:2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3:2
\]
Answer: 3:2

Q16. The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 2:3. Together they complete a work in 6 days. In how many days can A alone complete the work?
A. 4 days
B. 11 days
C. 9 days
D. 15 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_relative_efficiency_percent
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
W=(2+3)\times 6
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A=\frac{30}{2}=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(2+3)6}{2}=15
\]
Answer: 15 days

---

Q17. Pipe A can fill a tank in 12 hours and pipe B can fill it in 18 hours. Pipe C can empty the tank in 36 hours. If all three pipes are opened together, in how many hours will the tank be filled?
A. 10 4/5 hours
B. 9 hours
C. 14 hours
D. 7 hours
Answer: 9 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_two_fillers_one_empty
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_f-r_e}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
r=1/12+1/18-1/36
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 9 hours

---

Q18. Machines A, B and C make 12, 15 and 18 parts per hour. If they run for 5 hours, how many parts are made?
A. 231 items
B. 223 items
C. 225 items
D. 281.25 items
Answer: 225 items
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_machine_breakdown_midway
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
O=\sum r_it_i
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
O=12\times 5+15\times 5+18\times 5
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
O=225
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O=(r_1+r_2+r_3)t
\]
Answer: 225 items

Q19. A and B jointly work for 4 days. A then leaves, and B completes the job. If A and B alone need 20 and 30 days, how many days does the work take?
A. 28 4/5 days
B. 24 days
C. 26 days
D. 20 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(9+6)\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{120}{6}=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 24 days

---

Q20. A alone takes 24 days and B alone takes 6 days. A works alone for 4 days, then B joins. How many days will the job take in all?
A. 12 days
B. 4 days
C. 10 days
D. 8 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=1\times 4,\quad R=24-4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{20}{1+4}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{24-1\times 4}{1+4}
\]
Answer: 8 days

---

Q21. A job earns ₹9000 for A and B. If their work shares are in the ratio 8:12, what is A's wage?
A. ₹4500
B. ₹3606
C. ₹3600
D. ₹3598
Answer: ₹3600
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_wage_partial_time
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
Share=\frac{c_i}{\sum c}\times W
\]
Use the work-share ratio for the wage.
\[
Share=\frac{8}{20}\times 9000
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
Share=3600
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
Share=contribution\ ratio\times wage
\]
Answer: ₹3600

---

Q22. 15 workers working 6 hours per day finish the job in 16 days. If the daily working time is 8 hours, how many workers are needed to finish it in 12 days?
A. 18 workers
B. 11 workers
C. 15 workers
D. 20 workers
Answer: 15 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
15\times 16\times 6=M_2\times 12\times 8
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
M_2=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{15\times 16\times 6}{12\times 8}=15
\]
Answer: 15 workers

---

Q23. A job needs 6 days of work. Work stops for 2 days during the job. How many days pass from start to finish?
A. 10 days
B. 4 days
C. 8 days
D. 9 3/5 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{60}{6+4}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+2=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 8 days

---

Q24. Food is sufficient for 30 people for 30 days. After 8 days, the number of people becomes 40. For how many more days will the food last?
A. 12 1/2 days
B. 16 1/2 days
C. 19 1/2 days
D. 24 3/4 days
Answer: 16 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_population_change
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{900-30\times 8}{40}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=16.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 16 1/2 days

Q25. A tank is filled by a pipe taking 12 hours and emptied by another pipe taking 36 hours. In how many hours will the tank be filled if both are opened?
A. 37 1/2 hours
B. 27 hours
C. 30 hours
D. 25 hours
Answer: 30 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 30 hours

---

Q26. After a delay, 80 of 300 records are completed by 8 workers. How many extra workers are needed if the remaining records must be finished in 8 days?
A. 15 workers
B. 19 workers
C. 20 workers
D. 25 workers
Answer: 20 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{220}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-8
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 20 workers

---

Q27. Starting with A, two workers alternate. A completes 6 records and B completes 4 records on their respective turns. How many hours will be required to complete 120 records?
A. 22 hours
B. 30 hours
C. 12 hours
D. 24 hours
Answer: 24 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_hours
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=6+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 24 hours

---

Q28. A, B and C together complete a work in 8 days. A and B together can complete it in 16 days. In how many days will C alone complete the work?
A. 40 days
B. 32 days
C. 38 days
D. 33 days
Answer: 32 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_unknown_worker_from_team
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_X=r_{team}-\sum r_i
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
r_X=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{16}-\frac{1}{32}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_X=32
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 32 days

---

Q29. A printing team completes 2 pages per day for the first 5 days. After that, more workers join and the team completes 4 pages per day. If 12 pages must be completed, how many days are needed in all?
A. 8 1/4 days
B. 1 1/2 days
C. 8 1/2 days
D. 5 1/2 days
Answer: 5 1/2 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_multi_phase_join_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
\sum r_it_i=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=2\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{2}{4}=5.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 5 1/2 days

Q30. A ration stock is enough for 24 people for 36 days. Find the number of days for 40 people?

\[

\[
24\times 36=40\times D_2
\]

D_2=\frac{24\times 36}{40}
\]

Q33. Two workers A and B have efficiencies 3:4. If both together finish the job in 12 days, in how many days can A alone finish it?

B. 19 days
C. 28 days
D. 9 days
Answer: 28 days

Family: tw_efficiency_ratio_scaling
Explanation:

\[
W=(a+b)T
\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(3+4)12}{3}=28
\]

Q36. A with B takes 10 days, B with C takes 12 days and C with A takes 15 days. How many days will A alone take?
A. 22 days
B. 18 days
C. 19 days
D. 24 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_pairwise_ab_bc_ac
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
2(A+B+C)=(A+B)+(B+C)+(C+A)
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
AB=10,\ BC=12,\ CA=15
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A=\frac{AB+AC-BC}{2}
\]
Answer: 24 days

---

Q37. A repair team completes 18 metres per day and daily damage removes 3 metres. In how many days will 150 metres be completed?
A. 12 days
B. 9 days
C. 10 days
D. 6 days
Answer: 10 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
T=\frac{150}{18-3}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=10
\]

r_X=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{20}-\frac{1}{40}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_X=40
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 40 days

Q74. A team completes 4 files per day for 4 days and then 39 files per day after a team change. How many days are required for 120 files?

\]

\[

\]

\]

\]

\]

\]

\[
A=30/6=5,\quad B=30/10=3
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{5+3}=3.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{6\times 10}{6+10}=3.75
\]
Answer: 3 3/4 days

Q111. A packing job has 300 boxes. If 3 men and 4 women work together, with each man doing 4 boxes and each woman 3 boxes daily, how many days will it take?
A. 8 1/2 days

T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}

\[
T=\frac{300}{3\times 4+4\times 3}
\]

T=12.5

\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 12 1/2 days

Q112. There are 720 ration packets in a camp. If 36 packets are used each day, how many days will they last?
A. 25 days
B. 15 days
C. 19 days
D. 20 days
Answer: 20 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_resource_rate_equivalence
Explanation:

\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720}{36}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Answer: 20 days

Q113. 12 equal workers begin a job. After 10 days, only 9 workers remain. By how many days is the work delayed?

\[

\]

\[
R=300-12\times 10
\]

\[
D=\frac{180}{9}-\frac{180}{12}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:

\]
Answer: 5 days

Q115. The efficiency ratio of A to B is 4:5. They complete the work together in 10 days. In how many days can A do it alone?

Q121. 10 workers working 8 hours per day finish the job in 15 days. If the daily working time is 6 hours, how many workers are needed to finish it in 20 days?

B. 15 workers

D. 14 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_farm_harvest_workers
Explanation:

\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]

\[

\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{10\times 15\times 8}{20\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q122. A and B can separately complete a work in 8 and 6 days. A is absent for the last 2 days and B is absent for the last 3 days. In how many days is the work completed?

D. 12 days

\[

\]

\[
T=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 6 days

Q132. A pipe can fill a tank in 12 hours. Due to a leak, the tank is filled in 17 hours. In how many hours can the leak empty the full tank?

B. 30 3/5 hours
C. 35 4/5 hours
D. 40 4/5 hours
Answer: 40 4/5 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{17}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=40.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 40 4/5 hours

Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720}{30}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Answer: 24 days

\[

\]

Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=20\times 4,\quad R=360-80
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{280}{20+15}=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{360-20\times 4}{20+15}
\]
Answer: 12 days

---

Q185. 10 workers finish a job in 15 days, working 8 hours daily. How many workers should work 6 hours daily to finish it in 20 days?
A. 10 workers
B. 14 workers

D. 13 workers

\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[

\]

\[

\[

\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q213. A takes 12 days to finish a work and B takes 6 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 9 days
B. 18 days
C. 6 days
D. 4 days
Answer: 4 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(12,6)=12
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=12/12=1,\quad B=12/6=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{12}{1+2}=4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 6}{12+6}=4
\]
Answer: 4 days

Subtract the work already completed.
\[
R=360-10\times 6
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
D=\frac{300}{8}-\frac{300}{10}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=T_a-T_p
\]
Answer: 7 1/2 days

Q222. For the same work, the efficiencies of A and B are 30:18. What is the ratio of their times?

B. 3:5
C. 48:18
D. 18:48

Difficulty: Easy

\]

\[
T_A:T_B=18:30

\]

Q223. A can finish a work in 30 days and B in 24 days. They work together for 5 days, then B alone finishes it. How many days are taken altogether?

W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2

\[
W_1=(4+5)\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.

T=5+\frac{75}{5}=20

\]

Q224. A alone takes 10 days and B alone takes 30 days. A works alone for 2 days, then B joins. How many days will the job take in all?

Q228. Pipe A is an inlet of 12 hours and pipe B is an outlet of 36 hours. If both are opened together, in how many hours will the tank be completely filled?
A. 16 2/3 hours
B. 13 1/3 hours
C. 10 1/3 hours
D. 8 1/3 hours
Answer: 13 1/3 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 hours

\]

Q268. One worker can do a job in 12 days and another can do it in 24 days. In how many days will they complete it together?
A. 12 days
B. 36 days
C. 8 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(12,24)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/12=2,\quad B=24/24=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{2+1}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 24}{12+24}=8
\]
Answer: 8 days

B. 18 days
C. 12 days
D. 13 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:

\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[

\]

\[
D_2=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{20\times 24}{40}
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q302. 10 workers have completed 80 of 360 forms. Only 12 days are left. How many extra workers are needed to finish on time?

Q308. Normally a pipe fills a tank in 10 hours, but with leakage it fills in 20 hours. What is the emptying time of the leak?
A. 18 hours
B. 10 hours
C. 20 hours
D. 15 hours
Answer: 20 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 20 hours

Q311. One worker can do a job in 6 days and another can do it in 30 days. In how many days will they complete it together?
A. 5 days
B. 36 days
C. 18 days
D. 6 days
Answer: 5 days

Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:

\[
W=LCM(6,30)=30
\]

\[
A=30/6=5,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{5+1}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{6\times 30}{6+30}=5
\]
Answer: 5 days

W_{cycle}=6+4+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 2/3 days

Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=6+4+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 2/3 days

---

Q370. A group is expected to complete a work in 15 days. It has already worked for 3 days. What fraction remains?

C. 4/1
D. 1/5
Answer: 4/5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:

\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]

\[
R=1-\frac{3}{15}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
R=\frac{12}{15}=4/5
\]

\[

\]
Answer: 4/5

\[
T=20
\]

\]

Q380. A and B can separately complete a job in 10 days and 30 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 20 days
B. 7 1/2 days
C. 40 days
D. 10 days
Answer: 7 1/2 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(10,30)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/10=3,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{3+1}=7.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{10\times 30}{10+30}=7.5
\]
Answer: 7 1/2 days

---

Q387. A and B start the same job. A alone takes 18 days and B alone takes 24 days. After 5 days only B continues. How many days are needed in all?

Answer: 17 1/3 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:

\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[

\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{37}{3}=17\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=5+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 17 1/3 days

Q388. A group needs 6 working days to complete a job. If there is a break of 3 days, how many days pass from start to finish?

\]

Q389. A food stock would last 20 people for 45 days. After 6 days, more people join and the total becomes 25. Find the further duration in days?
A. 37 1/5 days
B. 29 1/5 days
C. 39 days
D. 31 1/5 days
Answer: 31 1/5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_population_change
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{900-20\times 6}{25}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=31.2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 31 1/5 days

T=9\times \frac{9}{4}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=20.25
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=9\div \frac{4}{9}=20.25
\]
Answer: 20 1/4 days

Q408. A and B can separately complete a job in 30 days and 18 days. What is the time taken when they work together?

B. 48 days

Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:

\[
W=LCM(30,18)=90
\]

\[
A=90/30=3,\quad B=90/18=5
\]

\[

\]

\]

Q417. A and B jointly work for 4 days. A then leaves, and B completes the job. If A and B alone need 10 and 15 days, how many days does the work take?

D. 9 days
Answer: 9 days

Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]

\[
W_1=(21+14)\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{70}{14}=9
\]

\[
T=4+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 9 days

Q419. A food stock would last 20 people for 30 days. After 6 days, more people join and the total becomes 30. Find the further duration in days?

\]

\[
D=\frac{600-20\times 6}{30}
\]

\]

Q420. 64 of 300 forms are complete. 8 workers are available and 8 days remain. How many additional workers are required?

D. 22 workers
Answer: 22 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{236}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-8
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=22
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 22 workers

Answer: 3/4

Q482. A and B begin a job together. A alone needs 20 days and B alone needs 12 days. A leaves after 2 days. In how many days will the job be completed in all?
A. 14 4/5 days
B. 16 1/5 days
C. 7 4/5 days
D. 10 4/5 days
Answer: 10 4/5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(3+5)\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{44}{5}=10.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 10 4/5 days

Q489. If 30 people can use a food stock for 24 days, for how many days will the stock last for 30 people?
A. 28 4/5 days
B. 24 days
C. 25 days
D. 29 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
30\times 24=30\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{30\times 24}{30}
\]
Answer: 24 days
